And for the next two months I ended up settling shows every other night for the tour,” says Klein.

CEOGRFBeem guides a company — celebrating 60 years of business management — that services entertainment, athlete and ultra-high net-worth families around the world. “While my role has changed as the CEO … the business has also changed. Our clients are working and living across the globe, needing services beyond traditional business management such as IP rights valuation, production accounting, royalty and participation examinations and family office operations,” he says. “The business has changed from transaction and compliance to value-add services and more bespoke offerings.” Beem notes that technology should enhance and support advisers: “Can you imagine what it would be like if we still operated by fax, or did tax returns by hand, or typed out checks to mail, or didn’t have our handheld supercomputers that allow us to stay connected to each other?”

So it’s really got to be about cultivating that deep connection,” he says. His clients are also branching out on digital platforms, “choosing to do some studio work, do more co-writes, to do more producing, and going in and working with other artists” in order to make money — with the side effect of connecting with fans, too.

Given the recent upheavals in the entertainment industry, this trio practices what it preaches for its clients. They prepare before any big event happens, says Seth Gettleson. “We always like to make sure they have a year or two worth of living expenses available to them.” While some of their clients have experienced cutbacks as studios slash big overhead deals, “we try to stay flexible, and we always try to coach our clients to have the ability to be flexible,” says O’Connor. Harvey Gettleson reflects that when he started, his first big actor client had three rules to take on a project: “If you paid him the price that was more than what he made in the picture before, let him bring his RV instead of a trailer and he wasn’t going to be terribly embarrassed by your project.” But now, everything is more complicated. “I truly spend a third or more of my time negotiating investments and ways for my clients to get involved ina company.”

Freemark FinancialAfter a rough period in the film and TV business, what’s that funny feeling? Is it … optimism? “Recently it feels like there’s more momentum in the space than there has been in recent years,” says Meyer, whose clients include Mark Harmon, Ellen Pompeo and Jon M. Chu. “We’re seeing more things happening, which is great to see.” That’s not to say it’s all happening in the U.S. Meyer says that Spain has become the latest hot spot for clients’ productions — and as business managers that comes with a level of complexity in terms of taxes and incentives. “You need to plan very thoughtfully on what projects you move forward with,” he says. “We find ourselves being thought partners with clients now more than ever.

Founding principalForward Business ManagementCurry oversees a boutique firm that doggedly fights for its clients. I wake up every morning and there’s fires everywhere,” Curry says, adding that he’s also got a psych degree. “My job is to try and really sit someone down, figure out what they really want out of life, and just give them good solid advice, and then distill all the information that will help them get there because they’re going to get taken advantage of.” Curry notes that his job has become more expansive, and his firm is trying to create generational wealth. “You have to give advice, and you have to be part of the team in the C-suite that’s helping guide things.”

Focus Partners WealthThe husband-and-wife team are starting to integrate AI tools, such as Microsoft Copilot, into their work, while trying to navigate concerns around security and client privacy. “It’s still new,” Pam says. “But we believe that if we’re not in the forefront of this, we are going to not be able to compete with our competitors in the future.” In one case, it took a couple of hours to do some tax work with AI that would have taken three days without it. “It’s a game changer,” Amir says. As for unusual client requests, Amir will get involved in renovation jobs, recommending landscape and hardscape contractors. FBMMThe war in Iran has had all sorts of unexpected effects — including on touring artists. “The price of diesel is through the roof this summer,” Killian says. Romano says that festival artists are among the hardest hit as that part of the business slows down. CEOFBMMCheek’s firm boasts some of the biggest names in country music. Before this year, he had not heard the term “blue dot fever” — the contagion of unsold tickets that has hit the touring business as concertgoers have less discretionary income. “I do think pockets are tighter,” he says. “You can see that just in the effect of fuel going up and other things going up.” The biggest acts don’t have a problem — but it has hit the mid-tier artists. In some cases, the solution is to forgo arena bookings in favor of smaller venues. The firm is also taking a cautious approach as artists are offered AI deals: “How much money that means? I have no idea,” he says.

FounderEvolve Business ManagementCampos notes that modern business managers are strategists who must navigate the complexities of each client’s world. “Five years ago, much of our industry was focused on tracking money. DiPerna AssociatesDiPerna leads a team of 21 CPAs, accountants, bookkeepers and administrative personnel with a roster spanning Emmy, Grammy, Oscar and Tony nominees and winners. CRM ManagementVariety’s 2023 Business Managers Elite honoree has created deep relationships with her clients. “I have become so trusted an advisor to my clients and their families, that many clients’ children — now coming into their own talent and subsequent success — have made the decision to work directly with CRM. Business Wealth & Tax ManagementEvery day is unique for Shaheen — especially in a volatile market, like the one he has observed over the last few years — and he tries to get in front of changes impacting the industry. “Not that we have a crystal ball and can predict the future, but learning from what’s going on enables us to make better, more informed decisions,” Shaheen says. Bernstein Sports & EntertainmentThe sports and entertainment division of Bernstein Private Wealth Management has staff based in Nashville, Los Angeles and New York. Loot boxes, influencers, and addictive features face an EU crackdown

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The proposed Digital Fairness Act seeks to bolster consumer protections for online users.﻿

The proposed Digital Fairness Act seeks to bolster consumer protections for online users.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Gaming, social media, and e-commerce platforms are facing a fresh wave of sweeping European Union rules that aim to make “your online experience fairer.” The newly proposed Digital Fairness Act (DFA) published by the EU Commission on Wednesday is designed to tackle practices that exploit consumers into spending more of their time and money online, including misleading prices or availability, addictive design features like gaming loot boxes, undisclosed influencer marketing, and more.

“The Act will protect consumers of all ages shopping and browsing for goods or services online,” the EU Commission said in a news bulletin. “It will also mean fairer competition for businesses, which are expecting to save EU consumers up to €13.7 billion ($15.1 billion) a year.”

Here’s an overview of what the DFA proposal includes:

The scope of the restrictions being proposed is broad, and could have a significant impact on major parts of the online digital landscape, from the “rabbit hole” algorithms across YouTube, TikTok, and Meta platforms, to AI chatbots and apps like DuoLingo that relentlessly nudge users to keep interacting with them. Some gaming organizations have already slammed the DFA proposal, describing its restrictions around virtual currencies, loot boxes, and offering micro-transactions to minors as being “unworkable, unjustified and disproportionate.”

Given this is a draft proposal, the DFA will undergo various approvals through the European Parliament and the Council of the European Union before being passed into law, and its current measures could be changed or removed entirely as part of that process. When (or if) it does come into force, the EU Commission says companies who violate the regulations could see fines of up to six percent of their worldwide annual turnover.

“Our consumer protection rules will help ensure that businesses compete on the merits of their products and services - not on their ability to exploit consumers or evade the rules. We are not making it harder to do business. We are making it harder to do business unfairly,” EU Commissioner Michael McGrath said in remarks following the DFA draft publication. “We cannot have 21st-century consumers protected by rules that have not kept pace with the 21st-century economy.”

WhalesBot Sees Physical AI Educational Robots as a New Frontier for Hands-On AI Learning

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With EliteMind, WhalesBot creates a connected AI learning journey from digital learning to real-world practice.

SHANGHAI, Nov. 11, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- In September 2026, WhalesBot introduced EliteMind, a modular Physical AI educational robot. Set to be showcased at the WhalesBot 2027 Global Product Launch Event on December 4, 2026, EliteMind reflects the growing potential of Physical AI in hands-on education.

Physical AI educational robots will reshape how students learn AI. WhalesBot defines them as learning tools designed to bring AI learning into the physical world. By sensing their surroundings, interpreting information through AI models, and responding with physical actions, these robots make intelligent systems tangible and interactive. Through hands-on projects, students can program, test, and refine robot behaviors, gaining a deeper understanding of how AI works while building AI literacy.

In recent years, AI has become an increasingly important part of hands-on education. In 2023, UBTECH's UGOT combined AI vision and programming with modular robot building, while Unitree's Go2 EDU supported advanced education and research in AI-driven motion control, environmental perception, and autonomous navigation. As AI becomes more deeply embedded in everyday life, greater emphasis is being placed on developing AI literacy from an early age. Introduced in 2025, VEX AIM combines AI vision with robot programming, allowing students to explore how intelligent systems perceive their surroundings and make decisions. In 2026, FF Nova Mini paired a programmable humanoid robot with introductory Physical AI courses.

For WhalesBot, what sets EliteMind apart is the connected AI learning journey built around it. Through OceanLevel, WhalesBot's AI & STEM learning platform, students explore how intelligent systems work and develop their own AI models. W-Hong LLM provides the intelligence behind EliteMind, powering its perception, reasoning, interaction, and AI-driven creation. Students then bring their digital learning into the physical world with EliteMind, testing their ideas, observing real-world responses, and refining their creations through hands-on experimentation. Beyond the classroom, ENJOY AI competitions offer further opportunities to apply their knowledge, explore new ideas, and take on new challenges.

"We believe every young person should have the chance to understand AI, explore their own ideas, and learn to work alongside AI to build a better world," said Xufeng Fei, CEO of WhalesBot. Working with educators and partners worldwide, WhalesBot hopes to bring hands-on Physical AI learning to more young people, giving them the opportunity to turn their ideas into real-world creations.

Join the live online event to get a closer look at EliteMind and discover what's next for WhalesBot in AI & STEM education. Register now: https://docs.google.com/forms/d/e/1FAIpQLScShVPnIr1-YAS6Xu8xVeGgJOpTmlbH10_6Rxq6lZf7RkUydw/viewform?usp=header

WhalesBot Technology, founded in 2018 and headquartered in Shanghai, China, provides STEM and AI education solutions for learners aged 3 to 22, with more than 1 million units sold in over 81 countries and regions. WhalesBot also sponsors ENJOY AI, an international STEM and robotics competition built around creative robotics and coding challenges.

WhalesBot, a global leader in STEM and AI education, has announced EliteMind, a modular Physical AI robot designed to bring AI learning into the...

From hands-on robotics and coding to AI learning and international competition, WhalesBot is connecting different learning experiences into a...

LA Card Show Is Bringing Collecting Closer to the Game

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From Dodger Stadium to Intuit Dome and now SoFi Stadium, founder Adam Derry is building a collectibles business around the places where sports history is made, connecting a growing marketplace with the culture, commerce and experiences that give its objects meaning.

At Dodger Stadium, collectors stepped away from rows of trading cards and memorabilia to throw pitches inside the same bullpen used by Major League players.

The Dodgers were approaching another postseason following back-to-back World Series championships, and baseball was still very much in season. Nearby, pitcher Will Klein practiced during his rehabilitation as children and longtime fans took turns throwing pitches in a part of the ballpark ordinarily reserved for professional athletes.

For LA Card Show founder Adam Derry, that proximity between the game and the collectibles marketplace has become the foundation of a growing business. After bringing collectors onto the Clippers' court at Intuit Dome and into the Dodgers' bullpen, his next stop is SoFi Stadium, where more than 750 vendor tables are scheduled for December 12 and 13, ahead of Super Bowl LXI.

The two-day event will include an on-field mini-camp for VIP attendees, extending a model built around access to the sporting environments where athletes compete and history is made.

At Dodger Stadium in September, more than 650 vendor tables filled the ballpark with sports cards, Pokémon, memorabilia and other collectibles. Beckett offered grading services, music filled the venue, and the Dodgers' road game against the Miami Marlins played on DodgerVision. Fans explored the dugouts and walked the infield, trading their familiar view from the stands for the perspective of the players.

"Am I supposed to be here?" Derry recalled thinking during an interview at the event.

For Derry, the connection between the collectibles marketplace and the places where sporting history unfolds is fundamental to the experience.

"Sports cards are a relic. They're an echo of moments that matter," he said.

For an industry long associated with hotel ballrooms and convention floors, LA Card Show represents a different approach to the business of collecting. Derry is bringing the marketplace into professional sporting environments, making their history and cultural significance part of the experience itself.

Before establishing LA Card Show, Derry built his career in fashion, creative direction and brand development. Through his Los Angeles agency, ADBD, he worked on projects connecting consumer products with the communities and cultural identities surrounding them.

"We weren't just creating a show," Derry explained. "We were creating a platform with a branded purpose."

That philosophy has shaped an expanding operation built around professional sporting venues, where the surroundings contribute something beyond exhibition capacity.

LA Card Show's April 2025 edition at Intuit Dome advertised more than 250 vendors. That September, approximately 350 vendors participated at Dodger Stadium, followed by more than 500 at Intuit Dome in March 2026.

The company expanded again at the Los Angeles Convention Center in June, where more than 700 vendor tables shared the event with fashion brands, entertainment and sports activations. By September, LA Card Show returned to Dodger Stadium with nearly 700 participating vendors, according to DodgerBlue.

SoFi represents its next major expansion, taking the marketplace into professional football with more than 750 advertised vendor tables across two days.

The progression reflects more than an increase in exhibitors. Derry has paired the marketplace's growth with increasingly ambitious access to the venues themselves.

"Dodger Stadium was top of our list, a place that we wanted to go and make sure that we built a relationship with," he said.

At Intuit Dome, attendees could walk through the Clippers' player tunnel and shoot on the NBA court beneath the Halo Board. At Dodger Stadium, the bullpen, dugouts and infield became part of the experience. The activities took place during the respective sports seasons, bringing collectors into facilities still actively associated with professional competition.

For Derry, access has become part of the commercial offering, alongside premium admission, exclusive merchandise and venue-specific programming.

"They trust us. They trust our community. They trust our structure that we've set up," he said of the Dodgers.

The partnership illustrates a different approach to event production. Conventional exhibition venues accommodate the marketplace. Professional stadiums can contribute the sporting history, physical experiences and cultural identity surrounding the objects being traded.

"Even watching the games has changed," Derry said, describing how entering the dugouts and bullpen affected his relationship with the ballpark.

The experience connects personal memories with the sporting history that collectors preserve through cards and memorabilia.

The sports-card market has always moved in conversation with the games themselves. A breakout season can ignite demand for a rookie card. Championships and career milestones can renew interest in memorabilia tied to a player or franchise. Scarcity, condition and collector sentiment shape prices, but sporting performance remains a powerful source of attention.

That relationship now operates within an increasingly sophisticated commercial infrastructure. Auction houses, grading companies, authentication services, pricing platforms and independent dealers support a marketplace where objects are tracked, evaluated and exchanged with growing financial precision.

During the interview, Mantel contributor and SOMOS Index founder Sam Goodman pointed to platforms such as Card Ladder and the increasing availability of historical sales data.

"I think it's become a bit of the backbone, to be honest," Derry said of the technology supporting the market.

Comparable sales and historical pricing have introduced the language of financial markets into collecting, changing how buyers and sellers evaluate the cards they own.

"They're basically trading like stocks," Derry said.

Yet for all its financial sophistication, the culture surrounding collecting has never been confined to cardboard.

Sports fandom extends through jerseys, sneakers, music, food, fashion and the merchandise people wear, display and preserve. These interests share audiences and cultural references, often shaping what people consider worth owning.

At Dodger Stadium, hip-hop artist Westside Gunn joined the September event for a meet-and-greet presented by R&G Records and Producer Plug, with a performance also reported in post-event coverage. Producer Large Professor was among the musical talent Derry highlighted, while memorabilia partner Legends' Attic brought former Dodgers All-Star Shawn Green and seven-time NBA champion Robert Horry for autograph appearances.

Westside Gunn's presence was especially fitting. Beyond Griselda Records, the Buffalo rapper has built ventures spanning fashion, contemporary art and professional wrestling through Fourth Rope, reflecting a culture where creative identity, sports fandom and collectible merchandise frequently intersect.

That same sensibility appeared at LA Card Show's June edition at the Los Angeles Convention Center, where brands including HYPLAND, Holiday, Vandy The Pink and Research Vintage participated alongside collectibles vendors and professional sports activations.

For Derry, these connections reflect the way people already participate in culture, rather than separate interests being assembled for an event.

"I think these things are intrinsically already together," he said.

The crossover extends to the objects themselves.

Derry recalled artist David Choe visiting an earlier show and designer Dr. Romanelli attending the September event. Contemporary artists have also explored the trading-card format as a smaller creative canvas, bringing artwork into a market built around physical ownership, authentication and resale.

Goodman also pointed to the different ways artwork circulates through traditional galleries and the trading-card market.

"Instead of being hung up in a museum or in someone's living room, they can change hands at a higher volume and speed," Goodman observed.

The same curatorial logic extends to the dealers assembling display cases. Each selects inventory around athletes, historical moments, aesthetics and rarity, presenting a personal interpretation of what deserves attention.

"That's what it takes until you can come here and see a whole bunch of curators at once," Derry said.

For all the attention paid to comparable sales and financial performance, he remains interested in the relationship between an object and its owner.

"This is art. Full stop. You're buying art. Do you love it? Does it connect with you?"

The value of a collectible is not exhausted by its latest transaction price. LA Card Show reflects a marketplace where cultural appreciation and commercial exchange operate together, bringing athletes, artists, musicians, creative brands and independent dealers into the same environment.

A Dodgers fan might attend for the bullpen. A Clippers supporter might arrive for a chance to shoot on the team's court. Someone following an artist or designer may discover the collecting marketplace through an interest that began elsewhere.

For an industry whose specialized vocabulary can intimidate newcomers, those experiences provide familiar points of entry.

"There's got to be bridges or no one can enter," Derry said, describing the complexity of pricing, comparable sales and market terminology.

Beyond the spectacle is a substantial marketplace of independent businesses, many built by entrepreneurs balancing collecting with other careers.

"We're creating these massive entrepreneurial moments," Derry said.

He described vendors maintaining full-time employment while sourcing inventory, preparing for shows and developing collecting businesses during evenings and weekends.

The event brings those sellers into direct contact with buyers who can examine inventory, negotiate transactions and build relationships with specialists across different collecting categories. Stadium access and cultural programming create opportunities to reach audiences beyond established hobbyists.

The demand to participate extends beyond the available exhibition space. Derry reported approximately 4,200 prospective exhibitors on a waiting list for future events, reflecting interest from independent sellers seeking access to the company's growing marketplace.

That demand indicates exhibitor interest rather than independently verified transaction volume or profitability. The commercial opportunity lies in connecting established collectors and independent dealers with audiences drawn by professional sports and cultural programming.

The range of participation remains broad. Experienced collectors pursue scarce inventory using increasingly sophisticated market information, while others acquire objects tied to childhood memories, favorite players or cultural interests.

Derry sees a parallel with the identity surrounding professional sports teams.

"I think you could argue that we're literally doing the same thing," he said when asked how LA Card Show fits into the broader fan experience.

Fans wear team jerseys as an expression of belonging. Derry wants the LA Card Show identity to carry a similar connection, supported by merchandise, recurring events and a community that extends beyond any single transaction.

LA Card Show's upcoming SoFi Stadium edition brings its largest announced marketplace into professional football during the NFL regular season, two months before the venue hosts Super Bowl LXI.

Scheduled for December 12 and 13, the event will feature more than 750 vendor tables spanning sports cards, memorabilia, trading card games, fashion, toys and other collectibles. Programming includes music, special appearances, grading services and limited-edition releases, alongside an exclusive VIP on-field mini-camp.

The setting carries significance beyond its scale.

In February 2022, the Rams defeated the Cincinnati Bengals at SoFi Stadium to win Super Bowl LVI on their home field. Five years later, the stadium will host another championship, bringing the NFL's biggest game back to Inglewood.

LA Card Show's December event arrives between those moments, placing collectors on a field that has already produced a championship and is preparing to host another.

The move also completes a progression across three professional sports. Baseball offers the bullpen and infield. Basketball opens the court and player tunnel. Football brings participants onto the playing surface itself.

Each venue contributes its own sporting identity, while the business remains anchored in independent commerce, cultural programming and access to experiences normally associated with professional athletes.

Derry intends to carry that model beyond Los Angeles.

"We're trying to be away game versions of our show, but as LACS," he explained.

His ambition reflects a background in brand development rather than conventional event promotion. LA Card Show is building an identity intended to travel through partnerships with sporting venues, regional collecting communities and creative collaborators.

He also envisions larger multiday festivals incorporating fashion, food, music and entertainment, developing an experience that reaches beyond any single collecting category without abandoning the dealers and collectors at its center.

For Derry, that ambition remains grounded in the motivations that brought collectors into the market long before pricing platforms and portfolio tracking became commonplace.

"It's about the love of the game," he said, describing how financial analysis and personal attachment can coexist within the same collecting culture.

Collectors can search for rare inventory, negotiate trades and meet dealers at shows throughout the year. Far fewer opportunities allow them to throw pitches inside the Dodgers' bullpen, shoot on the Clippers' court or step onto an NFL field preparing to host the Super Bowl.

A card show brings collectors closer to the objects they want to own. LA Card Show is bringing them closer to the places that made those objects worth collecting.

As AI model launches put Kubernetes inference on a deadline, CoreWeave deepens its open-source involvement

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cloud providers are scaling Kubernetes inference to serve frontier AI models, and the problems they encounter in production are helping shape the open-source projects they rely on.

CoreWeave Inc. sits near the center of that shift as AI agents reshape enterprise infrastructure. The company describes itself as a cloud built from the ground up for AI rather than retrofitted for it. Much of its growth traces back to the open nature of the container orchestration platform and the ecosystem around it, according to Yoshi Tamura (pictured, left), principal product manager at CoreWeave. CoreWeave has now upgraded to Platinum, the Cloud Native Computing Foundation’s highest corporate membership tier, to help shape how Kubernetes handles the large AI workloads its customers run.

“Without [Kubernetes], I don’t think we would’ve been able to get this kind of explosive growth,” Tamura said. “With this upgrade to Platinum, we indicate that we really mean it. We want to give back to the community. We want to be more involved.”

Tamura and Rita Zhang (right), principal software engineer at CoreWeave, spoke with theCUBE’s John Furrier and Rebecca Knight at KubeCon + CloudNativeCon NA, during an exclusive broadcast on theCUBE, SiliconANGLE Media’s livestreaming studio. They discussed why CoreWeave upgraded to CNCF Platinum membership and what it takes to run Kubernetes inference in production. (* Disclosure below.)

Open source can get a model into a cluster, but that doesn’t guarantee it will perform at scale. CoreWeave’s inference team brings together infrastructure specialists and machine learning engineers, working with model labs and open-source inference engines to patch engines and runtimes for newly released models, according to Zhang.

“Running in production is hard,” Zhang said. “We work a lot with the open-source community, not just on the inference stack, but also with the model providers. Just because it’s on Hugging Face and you download the weights doesn’t mean it will work per your requirement. This is where we work very closely with the labs and the open-source community — like vLLM, SGLang — to make sure that for day-zero models we actually are able to patch the engines and provide our own kernels and patch the runtime.”

Kubernetes special interest groups rely on users to bring them production problems, such as finding nodes with the right devices and accounting for cluster topology when scheduling large training and inference jobs, Zhang explained. CoreWeave remains smaller than incumbent cloud providers and relies on open source more than many realize, Tamura added. Its work with leading AI model builders now gives it an early look at where frontier workloads are headed.

“We’re in a very unique position,” Tamura said. “Maybe we can actually find a way to kind of bring it back to the community and sort of lead which direction Kubernetes, a larger community, could go.”

Stay tuned for the complete video interview, part of SiliconANGLE’s and theCUBE’s coverage of KubeCon + CloudNativeCon NA.

(* Disclosure: CoreWeave sponsored this segment of theCUBE. Neither CoreWeave nor other sponsors have editorial control over content on theCUBE or SiliconANGLE.)

Alicia Zhu Joins Live Nation-Backed Creator Nation As Chief Operating Officer

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Alicia Zhu has been hired for the role of Chief Operating Officer at Creator Nation, the Live Nation-backed creator company co-founded by Scott and Alexis Fisher.

Launched in October, Creator Nation is a platform focused on acquiring and scaling creator-management firms and adjacent businesses. Their thesis is that, while creators have already proven they can build massive audiences, the infrastructure around them has not yet fully caught up — and they can be a part of correcting that.

With Live Nation as its capital partner and strategic backer, Creator Nation will support creators across content, commerce, brand partnerships and live experiences. Marking their first executive hire, Zhu oversees financial operations, strategic initiatives and mergers and acquisitions as Creator Nation builds its portfolio of creator-focused businesses. Her appointment was effective November 9. Related Stories News 'Stick' Soundtrack: All The Songs You'll Hear In The Apple TV+ Golf Series Festivals Santa Barbara Film Festival Awards: Anne Hathaway Set For American Riviera Honor

“Creator Nation is being built for the next generation of creator-led businesses, and Alicia brings exactly the strategic and operational experience we need at this stage,” said Scott Fisher, Co-Founder and President of Creator Nation. “She has a strong track record of partnering with management teams, identifying growth opportunities and helping businesses scale. Her leadership will be central as we build Creator Nation’s platform and expand the ways we support creators.” Watch on Deadline

Zhu joins after seven years at Shamrock Capital, where she focused on investments across the creator economy and marketing-services space, partnering with management teams to drive business growth. Prior to joining Shamrock in 2019, she worked in Corporate Strategy at The Walt Disney Company, focusing on M&A and long-term strategic initiatives. She graduated cum laude from Harvard University and earned her MBA with honors from Harvard Business School.

Zhu told us, “Creators are building businesses that extend far beyond a single platform or revenue stream. To grow those businesses sustainably, they need capital, operating expertise and the right partnerships. Creator Nation will bring those resources together across content, commerce, brand partnerships and live experiences. I’m proud to join the team at such an important moment.”

Scott and Alexis Fisher came to Creator Nation with firsthand experience helping talent turn digital attention into television, consumer products, tours and enduring fan businesses. Scott previously founded Select Management Group, which grew into one of the largest creator-management companies before its acquisition by Propagate Content last year. Select reps more than 500 creators with a combined audience of over one billion, and he has helped build businesses beyond traditional management, including co-producing Hulu’s The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives and developing consumer brands such as Hairitage and Sacheu. Alexis founded Timeline Management, where she has built careers and businesses across creator management, branding, music and touring. Her work has helped bring creator-driven properties including the Dallas Cowboys Cheerleaders, the Secret Lives of Mormon Wives cast and Gracie’s Corner into live experiences.

All Winners At The Golden Joystick Awards 2026

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

Folks, awards season is here. Buckle in as the gaming world celebrates the year's biggest hits. First up are the Golden Joystick Awards, taking place right now. There's a huge array of games up for Ultimate Game of the Year in a race too close to call, with Resident Evil Requiem, Dispatch, Control Resonant, and 007 First Light among the favorites.

While it's unlikely we'll have a repeat of Clair Obscur's sweep last year, although GTA 6 may just have a chance, we'll have you covered every step of the way. Here are the winners as they happen at the Golden Joystick Awards.

In honor of Movember, your gameplay supports men’s health. From November 11 to 30, for every game played, Google will donate $11 to Movember, up to $500,000. Every play helps drive awareness and funding for men’s mental health, suicide prevention, prostate cancer, and testicular cancer.

It looks like Cavill used Google Antigravity and other AI tools on the HP Googlebook 14 to “translate natural language prompts into working 16-bit game mechanics.”

2 Decades Later, Stephenie Meyer’s Abandoned Twilight Novel Got An Unexpected Comeback

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Twilight fans got some unexpected but welcome news when Netflix announced its new animated series based on Midnight Sun. This novel dives back into the original story of book one, but from Edward Cullen's perspective. And if that isn't wild enough, the book's journey to publication is a fascinating saga in its own right.

Love it or hate it, Twilight has an enduring presence in pop culture, solidified in part by its live-action film adaptations. The movies have spawned entire social media trends, including dubbing fall "hoa hoa" season after the soundtrack. So, it's not too surprising to see Netflix attempting to recreate the magic by bringing a new version of Stephenie Meyer's series to life. Along with the pivot to animation over live action, Midnight Sun is an interesting project for many reasons. That includes the book's own complicated origin story.

Officially, Midnight Sun released in 2020, treating fans to a fascinating look into Edward's perspective on the events of Twilight. For newer fans, this may well have seemed like an exciting resurgence out of nowhere. But those who've been with the series from the beginning may remember that we once almost had Midnight Sun nearly two decades earlier.

Once upon a time, the Twilight companion novel was supposed to release during the franchise's initial wave of popularity. Stephenie Meyer actually started writing the book around 2003 as part of her work on Twilight. Initially, it likely would've come out not long after the original trilogy wrapped up with the 2008 release of Breaking Dawn. However, those plans got derailed in a big way that same year.

In 2008, the first twelve chapters of an early draft of Midnight Sun were posted online without Meyer's consent, giving fans an unplanned preview of the work in progress. She addressed the leak on her website, calling the leak, "a huge violation of [her] rights as an author." In that same missive to fans, she put the story on indefinite hold, saying it felt like there was no possible way for her to continue writing it in her current frame of mind. We don't know exactly who leaked the story or why, but it's likely that voracious fan enthusiasm for the series at the time was a direct contributor to the incident.

Meyer claimed she never wanted fans to see the draft in that form, since it was unfinished and not ready for public release. However, she posted the manuscript that leaked on her website to let curious readers experience it without having to track down the illegally distributed version. For a long time, it seemed like those messy, incomplete initial chapters would be the end of Midnight Sun's story. Fans eager to see the story from Edward's perspective could see Meyer's early efforts at the story, but the full Midnight Sun we once expected sat as an unfinished manuscript for nearly 20 years.

Following Midnight Sun's indefinite hiatus, Meyer continued to work on other projects, including entirely new stories like The Host and The Chemist. Though she returned to the well of her original vampire trilogy a few times throughout the years, for all fans knew, we'd never get Midnight Sun. But then, in 2020, the Twilight author made a shocking surprise announcement. She had, in fact, finished writing Midnight Sun, and it would come out that very year. Over a decade after she abandoned it, Midnight Sun finally hit shelves as a complete, finished novel. Now, it may be about to rope in a whole new generation of fans.

Though Midnight Sun sold quite well when it finally arrived in 2020, it would've been interesting to see how things shook out if it debuted during the initial hype around Twilight. After all, if things had gone to plan without the leak, the alternate POV story probably would've come out before the original series of movies wrapped up. But, thanks to the leak, we'll never know how the story would've hit so close to its original source material.

Though we will likely forever love some thirsty teenage vampires, the cultural and literary landscape has changed quite a bit since Millennials flocked to their favorite bookstores for Breaking Dawn midnight release parties. That made the broader reception of Midnight Sun pretty interesting in and of itself, before Netflix even got involved.

Leaked draft drama aside, Midnight Sun is at its core a retelling of Twilight. Readers already know what happens, but this time, we get to see it all through Edward's eyes. And therein lies the delight - and the problem. For longtime fans, it's a treat to see things from another point of view. But it also makes Edward harder to love, thanks to his slightly insufferable internal monologue. It also highlights many of the same issues about his dynamic with Bella that have caused many readers to re-evaluate their relationship to the series over the last decade or so. That's given Midnight Sun an interesting place among the broader fandom, and makes it a fascinating place for Netflix to start for an adaptation.

Animation frees the project from many of the live-action film's limitations. Sparkly skin, werewolves, and the like will be easier to render realistically in animated form, letting Netflix's team lean into the action in ways the original movie couldn't dream of. But as a story, Midnight Sun is interesting because of its perspective more so than its actual events. And that's where things get interesting. The show is set to run for two seasons, offering plenty of space to retell the events of Twilight with more nuance than the 2008 film. But to really adapt Midnight Sun, the showrunners will still need to navigate the Edward internal monologue of it all.

If they pull it off, the Midnight Sun adaptation could easily make the once-abandoned manuscript even more popular than it was upon release. It could fan the flames of the simmering Twilight resurgence, introducing a new generation of fans to the series. Or, it could be another fiasco for the story years after the draft leak broke the internet (or at least, Stephenie Meyer's website).

Edward's perspective was alienating even for longtime Twilight fans when the book finally released. It will be a tough balancing act to present it in the show without making Edward seem like a monster or, perhaps even worse for the show's long-term viewership, an insufferably angsty ancient teenager. But cutting out those insights that make Midnight Sun stand apart from Twilight could mean Netflix reinventing the wheel rather than crafting something new.

That said, it's been 18 years, so fans might not be mad at a TV series that basically just retells Twilight with a few more vampire bits, either. Either way, the story that readers once thought we'd never see in full is about to hit a whole new audience when the Netflix adaptation finally arrives in the next few years.

Twilight marks the first movie in The Twilight Saga, centering on the forbidden love between ordinary human Bella Swan and the centuries-old vampire Edward Cullen.

New Skyrim DLC Officially Released For 15th Anniversary

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For the past 15 years, The Elder Scrolls 5: Skyrim has been the only mainline entry in the storied Elder Scrolls franchise. It's hard to believe the sixth iteration still isn't out, but Bethesda has never been a developer that sticks to clear timelines. You'd think Skyrim came out last week given how popular it still is (whether it's vanilla or heavily modded), but with its 15th anniversary this year, fans are celebrating a bit more often than usual.

That includes the new 15th anniversary drop officially announced by Bethesda; those same fans can celebrate in style. It's a pretty significant addition, so even if you haven't booted up your copy in a while, this might be the time to.

In a weird move coinciding with the exact 15-year anniversary of Skyrim down to the day, Bethesda has provided a new overview of a Creation bundle, which lets players get a steep discount on a collection of in-game Creations.

Here's a quick overview of the bundle from Bethesda:

"This bundle includes five of the best Creations to celebrate the anniversary of the game. Continue your adventure with Orc-filled quests, Dwarven guneons, a Dwemer ancient weapon, an upbeat follower, and a cozy home. To celebrate this momentous anniversary, the Skyrim Anniversary Bundle gathers five Creations from our Verified Creators, each offering a new reason to return. From finding a cause worth fighting for and meeting a new companion to exploring forgotten depths, wielding powerful weapons and discovering a new place to call home."

Consider this a curation of sorts from Bethesda, who are offering this bundle as a "mod suite" for players who don't want to hunt for the latest mods. Out of all of these, Kaitryn Fair-Shadows, a fully-fledged companion, is arguably the most impressive: "Voiced by Chloe Elmore, Kaitryn reacts to Skyrim’s main quest, DLC main quests, every guild questline and Daedric quest, select side quests and even specific world details like your current location and the weather. She also has her own approval system and can be romanced and married."

Echoes of Adventure is another adventure bundle:

Then there's the Kinggath Creations Bundle:

In all, the bundle is being sold for 1500 Credits, though individually it goes for 3,000 Credits. Consider this a sale of sorts, and Bethesda warns that the Anniversary Bundle will leave the Creation menu on December 12. That goes for all of the above bundles, actually.

Skyrim has lived on beyond what anyone could have hoped for. Morrowind and Oblivion have their fans (I'm partial to the former, as my favorite Elder Scrolls creation myself), but Skyrim came at the perfect time, when tons of people were looking for their first open-world game. It captured an entire generation, and it continues that legacy through fan creations, while we wait for the next mainline game to hit.

Helsing launches Clarity, a European rival to Palantir’s Maven

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Helsing Clarity runs hundreds of military AI agents on servers that customers own. In Washington, Palantir CEO Alex Karp told Politico he has been a Democrat his whole life.

Gundbert Scherf, co-founder and co-CEO of Helsing, at the launch of Clarity at Kraftwerk in Berlin

German defence company Helsing has launched Clarity, a direct challenge to Palantir. The AI platform helps armed forces turn drone, satellite and sensor data into targets. Helsing says it builds the software in Europe, under European control.

Clarity pulls in sensor feeds, open sources, written reports and the systems a unit already runs. It finds military assets and positions across them and shows them to a human for review. It then prepares the information a weapon needs, Helsing said in a statement on Wednesday.

Reuters reported that Palantir’s Maven leads this market. Maven already sits inside Pentagon and NATO workflows. Helsing did not disclose whether any military already uses Clarity, or when it expects it to enter service.

“All armed forces need an intelligence capability that keeps pace with the fight. Autonomous systems play an increasingly important role. It is therefore important to connect what is seen to what is struck,” said Gundbert Scherf, Helsing’s co-CEO and co-founder.

Helsing Clarity runs hundreds of AI agents built for military use, the company said. They share one military ontology, a common map of how the data fits together, and one evidence base. Helsing trained the models on real operational data, synthetic data made by AI and simulated environments.

The company said humans stay in command. An operator reviews the evidence, selects each asset to engage and authorises every hand-off to a weapon. Clarity nominates a target only when independent sources agree, confidence is high and an operator confirms. Clarity records every decision with the name of the person who made it.

The platform runs on infrastructure the customer owns. It follows NATO standards and the modular open systems approach (MOSA), so it can connect to weapons from any maker.

Helsing is pitching Clarity at European governments that want control over their military software. France’s domestic intelligence service is already dropping Palantir for a homegrown tool. France and Germany have also pledged to build a European rival.

In the UK, Palantir lobbied a health minister to consider extending its software to GP records, the Guardian reported. The campaign group Foxglove obtained the WhatsApp messages under the Freedom of Information Act. Palantir’s £330m contract to run the NHS data platform excludes GP data. Palantir told the Guardian that it is for the government to decide how to roll out the software.

In Washington, Palantir is preparing for a Democratic-controlled Congress. Chief executive Alex Karp spoke to Politico’s Playbook on Wednesday, a week after the midterm elections.

“I’ve been a Democrat my whole life. I grew up in a far left environment,” Karp told Politico.

He said all of Palantir’s products are “essentially progressive”. Karp also said Trump had mishandled immigration enforcement, which Palantir’s ImmigrationOS software supports under a $30m contract, according to Politico.

Politico reported that Palantir has hired Jon Tester, a former Democratic senator from Montana, as an adviser. The company faces possible congressional investigations. Democratic lawmakers have asked whether Palantir’s work with the Department of Homeland Security enables surveillance of Americans. The company has denied conducting or enabling mass surveillance.

“Be transparent, be forthright, address the question straight up,” Tester said of his advice to Karp.

On Monday, President Donald Trump nominated Michael LoSapio to lead the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA). CISA is the US civilian cyber defence agency. LoSapio has been Palantir’s chief information security officer since September 2024.

CISA has had no Senate-confirmed director since Jen Easterly left in January 2025, Nextgov/FCW reported. Its workforce has shrunk by about a third. If the Senate does not confirm LoSapio before this Congress ends, Trump would have to nominate him again. The new Senate will have a Democratic majority.

After being laid off by Microsoft, former Doom devs found new Smash TV-influenced FPS studio that isn't "subject to investor demands"

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Their first project: televised deathgame shooter Danger Pay

Back in July, Doom developers id Software laid off around a hundred people as part of wider Microsoft blood-letting. Now, a few of those devs have founded a new employee-owned studio, Hidden Horses. Their first project is Danger Pay, a single-player FPS influenced by ancient arcade blaster Smash TV (pictured) and dystopian movies such as deadly gameshow fable The Running Man – itself an influence on Smash TV.

Hidden Horses have 20 id Software alumni on the books, including former lead VFX artist Todd Boyce, principal engine programmer Dustin Land, senior AI and gameplay engineer Daniel Young, producer Andrew Willis, senior technical designer Jordan Turner, principal technical designer Tim Locke, and senior skybox artist Allison Dombek. They're looking to make "the kind of old school shooters that we think people want more of," in Land's words.

All members of Hidden Horses own a share of the company, with bosses "democratically" appointed, in the words of a FAQ. "Our structure is quite flat, and we elect leaders when needed," Willis told Vertical Slice in a separate interview. "Financially, this means we are not subject to investor demands and the preservation of the company and the jobs it sustains is paramount over needless growth and our 'capital' doesn't compete for returns with other potential investments."

All of which is obviously gesturing towards the horse elephant in the room: Microsoft, who've made enormous cuts to their Xbox biz in their eagerness to further supercharge their $4 trillion market value.

"We've replicated some things, changed some things, and added others [from our id Software days]," Willis went on. "Overall, I think top-down hierarchy is something we all have bad experiences with—we also don't want to trend chase or make a game by focus group. We want to make the kinds of classic gameplay focused games that we would play ourselves. We think there is a market for that and our experiences with the gaming community in our past work history informs that."

Willis's hope is that the "flat" structure will motivate staff to work harder, because they feel like they have a meaningful say over the company's direction. "It allows all ideas to be considered and means we don't miss out on any contributions from our employees just because they don't sit high up in some arbitrary hierarchy," he said..

I'm cynical about "flat" company structures, myself. I think they are very often just an excuse for the biggest egos in a company to throw their weight around. It can work given a friendly initial mix of founders, but it gets complicated when you start hiring new people who don't have the same chemistry. Mind you, there are no plans to expand the company dramatically for the foreseeable. "We want to create a vehicle that allows us to make games for our livelihoods, not to make crazy profits and get 100x returns," Willis went on. I'm also a bit leery of the fact that Hidden Horses have popped up a Kickstarter, because I'm leery about crowd-funding in general, even when Peter Molyneux isn't involved. The studio founders are currently working on some promotional initiatives to drum up support, including a studio documentary and *squints* a promotional beer? Still, I'm happy to hear that at least a few Cacodemon wranglers jettisoned by the big M have landed on their hooves feet. I wish Hidden Horses the best of luck with it all. As for Danger Pay, proper screens, footage and release details are to follow.

After Birkin Backlash, Kylie Jenner Explained Why She Has So Many Of The Pricey Bags

The Perovskite Solar Cell Kraken Has Been Unleashed Upon The US

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Perovskite is a relatively inexpensive material that has attracted thousands of researchers since 2009, when a team of scientists in Japan first demonstrated its use in solar cells. They achieved an solar energy conversion efficiency of little more than 3%, but since science loves a challenge, that was just the beginning of the story. In the latest chapter, the US firms Heliene and Caeulux have just introduced a new perovskite-enabled solar module that hits the jackpot at up to 27% efficiency, and it is already in commercial production, no less.

So far in the renewable energy transition, silicon has shouldered much of the heavy lifting due to its excellent solar conversion properties. Still, silicon is relatively expensive material that demands a complex manufacturing process. Perovskite is the reverse. Based on a naturally occurring mineral, synthetic perovskites can be lab-grown and applied to solar cell fabrication in a high volume, high throughput process similar to roll-to-roll manufacturing and other solution-based methods (see lots more perovskite background here).

There being no such thing as a free lunch, “raw” perovskite is challenged by natural conditions, namely, humidity. Tweaking the formula with other, more durable materials can resolve that problem. Researchers have also developed a tandem solution, in which a perovskite layer rests upon a silicon layer, providing an efficiency boost while minimizing the additional cost.

In the US solar market, the sticky wicket is the current state of federal energy, trade, and tax polices. Still, the red-hot demand for solar energy in the US has motivated solar stakeholders to steamroll over those obstacles, attracting overseas investors (here’s one example) as well as new domestic ventures.

On the domestic side, the Corning-backed manufacturer Heliene has been focusing on silicon while the startup Caelux has tackled the perovskite solar cell challenge with a glass substrate that can be layered onto other materials in a solar module. Now the two manufacturers have combined forces, with Caelux’s perovskite-doped glass boosting the conversion efficiency of Heliene’s N-type solar cells by up to 20%.

The Caelux-Heliene mashup comes at a time when Congress has finally put the wheels in motion to streamline the permitting process for energy projects. The two firms already have a running head start. On November 10, the two firms announced that commercial-scale production has already begun on their Hybrid-Tandem™ solar module

“Manufactured by Heliene in Minnesota, the perovskite plus silicon technology combines Caelux’s Energy Producing Glass with Heliene’s established silicon module platform to construct a solar module with two separate power generation layers that deliver higher power density,” Heliene explains.

“One of the advantages of Caelux’s technology is that we can integrate its Energy Producing Glass into our existing manufacturing lines without having to build an entirely new production setup,” added Martin Pochtaruk, Heliene CEO and Founder, in a press statement.

“With both companies manufacturing in the U.S., we’re bringing together two fully domestic supply chains to advance what’s possible with American-made solar,” Pochtaruk emphasized.

Stay tuned for the next development. The new modules are now in production at Heliene’s manufacturing facility Minnesota, with deliveries set for 2027. That’s just for starters. Caelux and Heliene have already targeted 29% efficiency and up for their next-generation modules.

The conversion efficiency improvement is the gift that keeps on giving. In the rooftop solar sector, for example, high-efficiency solar cells enable developers to squeeze more electricity out of a given space, making a rooftop array more economical for smaller properties that are otherwise shut out of the market. The cost of a smaller, more effcient array can also ripple downwards in the form of savings on labor, hardware, and transportation expenses.

For rooftop solar in general, the boost in efficiency provides an opportunity to take full advantage of power sharing programs with local utilities, which enable solar owners to earn revenue and qualify for time-of-use discounts. The benefits can ripple into home energy storage systems and home EV charging as well.

On a larger scale, the space-saving angle can help alleviate land use conflicts that otherwise stymie solar development, particularly in the area of farm-to-solar conversion.

Although real estate development is by far the primary factor driving the loss of farmland in the US, solar opponents (looking at you, Brooke Rollins) have played the farm card to gin up resistance to solar development in rural communities. With efficiency improvements, the economic case improves for solar arrays on marginal lands and smaller parcels. That can be partcularly helpful in the community solar field, where solar arrays are typically smaller.

Additionally, the improved efficiency can help accelerate the uptake of agrivoltaic practices that balance energy-producing solar panels with grazing and croplands. These dual-use arrays can enable farmers to continue working their land while guaranteeing a revenue stream from the solar developer.

Furthermore, high-efficiency solar opens up new opportunities in the field of floating solar, where developers can make use of reservoirs and other human-engineered bodies of water instead of taking up space on land. By taking up less room on water for the same power generation capacity, floating solar can also squeeze into reservoirs where recreation and habitat conservation also compete for space.

Perovskite solar cells represent just one key breakthrough area in the US solar industry. Easing the federal energy permitting bottleneck is another. That can be a two-edged sword, enabling more fossil energy resources to flow through the pipeline. However, permitting reform could also prevent the fossil energy industry from running amok through federal energy policy.

Members of Congress are currently negotiating over the particulars of Senate Bill 5653, aka the “Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act. If enacted as envisioned by the solar industry, the reforms will put a crimp in Trump’s “American Energy Dominance” policy, which has placed new and burdensome restrictions on both wind and solar activity in the US while aggressively supporting fossil fuels and nuclear, alongside other renewables including biomass, geothermal, hydropower. Even energy storage has come in for a share of love from the Trump administration, further underscoring the remarkable exclusion of wind and solar from federal energy policy.

The trade organization Solar Energy Industries Association, for one, is urging members on both sides of the aisle to ensure that permitting reform focuses on fairness, predictability, and certainty. In a letter to the House and Senate, SEIA emphasizes that solar and storage developers “cannot finance multi-year, capital-intensive projects without the predictability and certainty needed to build projects and connect them to the grid.”

“According to researchers at Wood Mackenzie, solar and storage represent 70% of the new capacity added to the grid thus far in 2026. Yet $120 billion in clean energy investment is caught up in current permitting delays, let alone the impact of permitting challenges to future deployment,” SEIA adds.

Of course, even if such a bill passes both Houses of Congress, US President Donald Trump could exercise his veto power, and he has already threatened to make copious use of it during his last two years in office. Nevertheless, US Presidents come and go, and this one is scheduled to go in January of 2029 — peacefully this time, one hopes.

Image: The US perovskite solar cell specialist Caelux is combining with the startup Heliene to manufacture high efficiency perovskite-silicon modules in the US (cropped courtesy of Caelux).

Who Runs the Ransomware Group Qilin?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The cybercrime group Qilin has emerged as the world’s most active ransomware affiliate program, claiming at least 365 victim organizations in the past three months alone. But even top Russian hackers make operational security mistakes, and the administrator of Qilin has made a few key missteps over the last decade that all point to a 36-year-old man living in St. Petersburg, Russia.

Operating since September 2022, the Russia-based ransomware group Qilin is the currently the most industrious ransomware-as-a-service gang, according to a report (PDF) this month by the cybersecurity firm NCC Group (KrebsOnSecurity profiled the administrator of the second most active ransomware group — The Gentlemen — in June).

The Qilin ransomware administrator “Haise” advertising his affiliate program on the cybercrime forum RAMP. Image: Ke-la.com.

Qilin attracts affiliates to hack into companies and deploy ransomware by offering them at least 80 percent of any extortion amount paid by victims. Its members practice the “double extortion” model, stealing sensitive data before encrypting systems and threatening to publish the files online. Unlike most ransomware partnerships based out of Russia, Qilin is happy to team with English-speaking affiliates, and its leader has shared on Telegram that they’ve worked with ransomware groups like Scattered Spider and RansomHub.

The head of Qilin goes by the handle “Haise,” but they weren’t always known by this nickname. It is not uncommon for cybercriminals to change their handle periodically as an operational security (opsec) measure. Doing so is generally a smart move, because it helps to sever one’s ties to older identities that may not have been as careful on basic opsec, such as separating their real life identity from their cybercrime activities. But even those cautious criminals will sometimes have to reference their past monikers when applying for membership at a new forum. And forums eventually get hacked or seized. That is precisely what happened with Haise.

The cyber intelligence platform Intel 471 records that in June 2022, Haise applied for membership to RAMP, a closely-guarded Russian cybercrime forum that was created as a haven for ransomware affiliate programs. Intel 471 has indexed direct messages from RAMP (the forum was seized by the FBI in January 2026), and those missives show the RAMP administrator approved Haise’s application, referring to this user as Haise and by their Telegram username: Alexbigboss44.

Searching Intel 471 for Alexbigboss44 reveals this was the Telegram account name shared by the user Rjun on the Russian cybercrime forums Exploit and XSS two years earlier in 2020. The cyber intelligence platform Flashpoint finds Alexbigboss44 is a prolific participant in cybercriminal forums and Telegram channels, primarily focused on the illicit trade of traffic, logs, and corporate access [full disclosure: Flashpoint is an advertiser on this blog].

The Qilin administrator once used the nickname “Rjun” on several forums, and shared their Telegram handle as “Alexbigboss44.” Image: Ke-la.com.

Flashpoint says this user was engaged in high-volume acquisition of Facebook logs and Google Ads accounts to facilitate fraudulent advertising and gambling schemes, and that they functioned as a broker and operator — managing a network of traffickers and purchasing compromised data to fuel their operations.

The link between the Qilin administrator Haise and the much older Rjun and Alexbigboss44 accounts was shared privately earlier this month by IntelHawk, which describes itself as an AI-based platform capable of de-anonymizing threat actors based on historical usernames, stylometry and other signals.

IntelHawk founder and chief technology officer Leo Bogod said an analysis of these identities shows a progression from novice arbitrageur and credit card fraudster in 2018–2019 to a more experienced participant and organizer of complex cybercrime-related activities in 2020–2023, with increasing attention to malware, botnets, and payment infrastructure.

Where does this connection between these threat actor identities lead? Intel 471 finds that by the time Alexbigboss44 was applying for membership to RAMP, Rjun was already a longtime member of several other Russian crime forums, including Darkmarket, Verified, and Zloy. Intel 471 says Rjun signed up at all of these sites with the email address mail-30a@mail.ru.

The identity threat protection firm Spycloud found the email address mail-30a@mail.ru at one time used the password “Rjun132” [Spycloud is also an advertiser on this blog]. A search for that email at the breach tracking service Constella Intelligence shows that it appeared in records maintained by gosuslugi.ru, the official digital platform for state and municipal services in the Russian Federation.

Gosuslugi was breached in late 2025, reportedly exposing more than 20 million records with full names, birth dates, and mobile numbers. According to the leaked Gosuslugi records (via Constella), the address mail-30a@mail.ru belongs to 36-year-old Aleksey Mikhailovich Yakovtsev from St. Petersburg, Russia.

Flashpoint finds Haise has long used a Telegram account with the user ID 118329521, recording more than 5,000 messages from this user since 2018. Records from Russia’s education watchdog Rosobrnadzor reveal that Mr. Yakovtsev went to grade school in Murmansk and that he holds a master’s degree in computing and information systems.

A search on this Telegram ID in Constella shows it is linked to the Russian phone number +79602347275. Pivoting on that phone number in Constella reveals it is connected to the email addresses aleksey1990yakov@mail.ru and rjun123321@mail.ru. IntelHawk finds that +79602347275 belongs to Yakovtsev’s biological sister, and that Aleksey often used his sister’s email address when registering at different websites.

That phone number appears on several airline flight records at Russian travel agencies that have experienced a data breach in recent years, and those records show Aleksey Yakovtsev, born August 18, 1990, flew between St. Petersburg and Murmansk at least three times between 2013 and 2014.

More recent flight records show that while Mr. Yakovtsev has not traveled by plane outside of Russia, he does fly frequently between Saint Petersburg and Sochi. Often called the “Russian Riviera,” Sochi is Russia’s largest resort city, situated along the coast of the Black Sea and about 1,200 miles south of Saint Petersburg by plane.

Attempts to reach Mr. Yakovtsev via email and phone were unsuccessful.

Further reading: Gang Exposed, an anonymous Twitter/X account that has a history of successfully deanonymizing suspected ransomware actors, reached similar conclusions about Haise’s real-life identity, crediting IntelHawk’s platform for helping to connect the dots.

The Qilin ransomware admin Haise’s now-deleted Telegram account, advertising the affiliate program in July 2024. Image: justpaste.it/iosba.

FC 27 November FUT Developer Patch Notes

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Major changes are coming to FC 27 Ultimate Team, and EA Sports has shared ideas about what they intend to do to daily and seasonal content. This comes less than a week after Patch 1.2.0, which introduced major gameplay changes and a nerf to 95-depth.

The focus of this month’s Developer patch notes is related to the content we have been receiving in Ultimate Team. This includes access to promo cards, evolutions, and much more.

According to EA Sports, players have been spending more times with the promo cards they have in their collections. However, the developers want to increase the access to specific promo items without tinkering the overall balance. In simple words, we can expect to have more access to specific promo cards, and EA Sports has already introduced it as part of the ongoing RTTK promo.

It’s a known fact that evolutions have been extremely poor in this year’s cycle. They have been weak and lost the popularity of last year. According to EA Sports, evolutions have lost the impact they can have in squad building and developing our starting XI.

Hence, they have assessed the data and feedback, and there’s an opportunity to fine-tune evolutions to make them more rewarding. Hence, I believe that we will find better evolutions in the days and weeks to come, which would be welcome news for the community.

Continuing from the previous change, EA Sports wants to witness more diversity when it comes to squad building. While powered-up evolutions will play a role, there will be more diversity in promo cards. Certain changes are also planned in gameplay to spice up the meta, which will directly allow us to make our teams lot more varied.

Lately, there’s been a discussion that the streamlined SBCs no longer reward us with challenge packs. However, EA Sports has clarified that the potential value of these packs have been considered when pricing the SBCs.

In simple words, the fodder cost of these SBCs would have been higher had we received challenge packs like we used to do. While there are no current plans to bring back challenge packs, this could change eventually based on feedback and data.

Despite a significant rise in the number of coins you can earn from Champions games, EA Sports believes that there could be more improvement in this department. Any new rewards could also be based around the Champions Token stores and what players typically love to redeem from it.
